pytest.raises(OrchestrateDefinitionError) as excinfo:\n            attested(\n                \"review\",\n                command=\"review.sh\",\n                instructions=\"review the artifact\",\n                ack=\"agent\",\n                produces={\"x\": file_nonempty()},\n            )\n        assert \"sentinel\" in str(excinfo.value).lower()\n\n    def test_attested_with_semantic_check_accepted(self) -> None:\n        step = attested(\n            \"review\",\n            command=\"review.sh\",\n            instructions=\"review\",\n            ack=\"agent\",\n            produces={\"summary\": json_file()},\n        )\n        assert step.kind == \"attested\"\n\n\nclass TestReservedAttrCollision:\n    \"\"\"FLAG-004: produces names colliding with _StepHandle attrs are rejected.\"\"\"\n\n    @pytest.mark.parametrize(\n        \"reserved\",\n        [\"id\", \"kind\", \"command\", \"argv\", \"plan\", \"produces\", \"repeat\", \"instructions\", \"ack\"],\n    )\n    def test_reserved_attr_rejected(self, reserved: str) -> None:\n        with pytest.raises(OrchestrateDefinitionError) as excinfo:\n            code(\"x\", argv=_valid_argv(), produces={reserved: json_file()})\n        assert reserved in str(excinfo.value)\n        assert \"reserved\" in str(excinfo.value).lower()\n\n\nclass TestRepeatForEachCompilation:\n    def test_for_each_from_compiles_to_dotted_ref(self) -> None:\n        transcribe = code(\"transcribe\", argv=_valid_argv(), produces={\"transcript\": json_file()})\n        process = code(\n            \"process\",\n            argv=[\"echo\", \"x\"],\n            repeat=repeat_for_each(from_=transcribe.transcript),\n        )\n        p = plan(\"sample.foo\", [transcribe, process])\n        d = p.to_dict()\n        assert d[\"steps\"][1][\"repeat\"] == {\n            \"for_each\": {\"from\": \"transcribe.produces.transcript\"}\n        }\n\n\nclass TestRoundTrip:\n    def test_to_dict_round_trips_through_load_plan(self, tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n        transcribe = code(\"transcribe\", argv=_valid_argv(), produces={\"transcript\": json_file()})\n        cut = code(\"cut\", argv=[\"echo\", transcribe.transcript], produces={\"out\": json_file()})\n        p = plan(\"sample.foo\", [transcribe, cut])\n        payload = p.to_dict()\n\n        # Write payload byte-for-byte and re-validate via the public load_plan.\n        plan_path = tmp_path / \"plan.json\"\n        plan_path.write_text(json.dumps(payload), encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        loaded = load_plan(plan_path)\n        assert loaded.plan_id == \"sample.foo\"\n        assert len(loaded.steps) == 2\n        assert loaded.steps[0].id == \"transcribe\"\n        assert loaded.steps[1].id == \"cut\"\n\n    def test_duplicate_sibling_step_ids_rejected(self) -> None:\n        with pytest.raises(OrchestrateDefinitionError) as excinfo:\n            plan(\n                \"sample.foo\",\n                [\n                    code(\"dup\", argv=_valid_argv(), produces={\"a\": json_file()}),\n                    code(\"dup\", argv=_valid_argv(), produces={\"b\": json_file()}),\n                ],\n            )\n        assert \"dup\" in str(excinfo.value)\n"

        Idea:
Implement Phase 4 (Python DSL + verify helpers + author check/describe/new) of the ArtAgents orchestrator V1 design per docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md sections 10 and 14. Phase 4 scope: ship `artagents.orchestrate` Python DSL as the only committed authoring representation. Authors write `<pack>/<orch>.py` using decorators/helpers like `@orchestrator`, `code()`, `attested()`, `nested()`. Step references are typed (e.g., `step.notes` resolves a produces handle, typo-checked at definition). The DSL emits a validated definition equivalent to the JSON manifest schema. Add `artagents author compile` to write the JSON manifest into `<pack>/build/<orch>.json` (gitignored); the runtime hashes/pins this artifact. Add `artagents author check` (sub-second static validation: schema, every produces/requires reference resolves, every nested plan resolves, attested non-trivial produces have semantic checks, code argv to `artagents orchestrators run` is rejected). Add `artagents author describe` (pretty-prints DAG: step kinds, produces, requires, max_iterations, repeat config, estimated cost ceiling if cost hints present). Add `artagents author new <pack>.<name>` (scaffolds Python file + fixtures/<name>/ + golden/<name>.events.jsonl from a template). The verify helper library (artagents/verify/) is already shipped from Phase 3 — re-use file_nonempty, json_schema, json_file, audio_duration_min, image_dimensions, all_of as DSL helpers. Phases 1 (kernel), 2 (three step kinds), 3 (produces + repeat) are committed and shipped — build on top. Do NOT implement new lifecycle verbs (Phase 5). Keep additive: existing JSON manifests and python/command runtimes still work. Tests: typed step reference catches typos at definition; compile produces a valid manifest in build/; check validates references and rejects sentinel-only attested produces; describe pretty-prints expected DAG; new scaffolds correct structure; existing JSON manifests still load and run.

User notes and answers:
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-001 three step kinds: Task mode has exactly `code`, `attested`, and `nested` so execution, evidence, and delegation stay distinct.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-002 repeat body attributes: Iteration and fan-out are `repeat.until` and `repeat.for_each` body attributes so they apply uniformly to steps and nested bodies.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-003 plan naming: Runtime uses immutable `plan.json` instead of checklist naming because the structure is a tree with nested bodies and loops.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-004 event hash chain: `events.jsonl` uses plain `sha256(prev_hash + canonical_event_json)` with no HMAC because V1 needs an edit tripwire, not keyed tamper resistance.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-005 no index database: V1 ships without `.index.db` because file walking and grep over `events.jsonl` are adequate until measured otherwise.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-006 committed DSL: `artagents.orchestrate` Python DSL is the only committed authoring representation so source review happens in Python.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-007 generated build JSON: `<pack>/build/<orch>.json` is gitignored and root `.gitignore` excludes pack build outputs because runtime pins generated artifacts.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-008 per-project CAS: V1 uses only per-project `<slug>/.cas/<sha256>` storage because shared CAS adds coordination without current need.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-009 code boundary: `code` is deterministic argv, a strict superset of `RUNTIME_KINDS={python,command}`, may target `artagents executors run <name>`, and must not target `artagents orchestrators run`.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-010 nested boundary: Sub-orchestrator delegation is exclusively `nested` so the gate can hash and pin child structure.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-011 gate checks: The gate validates active run presence, plan hash, event chain, and incoming command match before dispatch and prints the exact recovery command on rejection.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-012 gate ordering: The gate runs before `_prepare_project_request()` and `thread_wrapper.begin_orchestrator_run()` so rejected commands create zero project-run side effects.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-013 provenance surface: `events.jsonl` is the single write surface for task-mode provenance while thread provenance remains in `runs/<id>/run.json`.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-014 lifecycle dispatch: Task-mode lifecycle verbs extend `artagents/pipeline.py:15` and the existing `artagents orchestrators` CLI remains unchanged.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-015 task kernel namespace: New kernel modules live in `artagents/core/task/` to isolate gate, events, active-run, and env logic from legacy runners.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-016 structure guardrails: `TOP_LEVEL_ARTAGENTS_DIRS` adds `orchestrate` and `verify`, with `tests/test_doctor_setup.py` updated so doctor accepts the new packages.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-017 task run env: `ARTAGENTS_TASK_RUN_ID` is the integration surface for orchestrator, executor, and hype child calls to attach to the parent task run.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-018 child output preservation: Task mode preserves child output dirs and mirrors hype artifacts under `runs/<task-run-id>/steps/<step-id>/produces/` while standalone behavior stays unchanged so `tests/test_project_runs.py` keeps passing.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-019 attestor identity: Agent attestations require `--agent`, human attestations require `--actor` matching `ARTAGENTS_ACTOR`, and self-acks are rejected.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-020 ack rules: `approve` advances, `retry` is only valid after verifier failure, `iterate --feedback` is only valid for `repeat.until=user_approves`, and `abort` ends the run.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-021 inline produces checks: Produces inline checks replace verifier substeps while `passes_test` remains a normal `code` step.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-022 semantic checks: `author check` rejects sentinel-only attested outputs because non-trivial artifacts need semantic validation.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-023 stop-hook preamble: Every `artagents next` output includes the prohibition preamble verbatim because repeated injection fights context decay.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-024 additive migration: Existing `OrchestratorDefinition` JSON manifests and `RUNTIME_KINDS={python,command}` keep working because V1 migration is additive.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-025 honor model: V1 is single-host and honor-based with strong logging because it does not defend against malicious local agents.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-026 cut points: Phase 3 is the frozen-plan runner ship point and Phase 5 is the author-friendly lifecycle ship point.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-027 canonical migration: `artagents/packs/builtin/hype/` is the canonical migration example because it exercises the existing executor pipeline and additive pack layout.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-028 phasing order: V1 implementation follows build phases 1-9 so kernel, step semantics, authoring, lifecycle, nudge, CAS, inbox, and golden tests land in dependency order.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-029 V1 infrastructure limits: V1 excludes daemon, web server, HMAC/keyed crypto, `.index.db`, and shared CAS so the implementation stays file-based, inspectable, and additive.

        Prior batch deviations (address if applicable):
        [
  "Module isolation choice for resolve_orchestrator: rather than fully bypassing sys.modules (the spec_from_file_location/exec_module pair still requires the loader to register the module by name), the implementation registers under a uuid-suffixed key (`_artagents_orchestrate_<qid>_<uuid>`) and removes it in a finally clause. This achieves the spec's intent ('without polluting sys.modules twice') by ensuring no two resolutions share state and by removing the entry post-import. A pure no-sys.modules approach is brittle when imported modules use relative-import patterns; the finally cleanup is the standard idiom.",
  "Advisory observation mismatch: executor claimed files not observed in git status/content hash delta: .megaplan/plans/implement-phase-4-python-dsl-20260504-2154/execution_batch_3.json, artagents/orchestrate/compile.py",

  "meta_commentary": "Phase 4 ships the only committed authoring surface for V1 task plans: a tiny Python DSL at `artagents/orchestrate/` plus a top-level `author` CLI verb. Phase 4 is strictly additive \u2014 no schema changes, no lifecycle verbs (those are Phase 5), and existing `orchestrator.yaml` JSON manifests must keep working. Critical execution notes for the gate-flagged concerns:\n\n- **FLAG-002 (private validator)**: do NOT import `_validate_plan` from `artagents.core.task.plan`. Round-trip the DSL payload by writing to a temp file and calling the public `load_plan(path)`. The plan text says \"call `_validate_plan`\"; this finalize overrides that to use the public symbol.\n- **FLAG-003 (scaffold collision)**: `artagents/packs/builtin/hype/` exists as a folder-orchestrator. `author new` must refuse to write `<pack>/<name>.py` when `<pack>/<name>/` already exists, not just when the same `.py` file exists. Python's package-shadows-module rule would otherwise make the scaffolded DSL unimportable.\n- **FLAG-004 (`__getattr__` collisions)**: produces names that collide with real `_StepHandle` attributes (`id`, `kind`, `produces`, `repeat`, `command`, `argv`, `plan`, `instructions`, `ack`) silently bypass the typo trap. Reject these at construction.\n- **FLAG-005 (nested cycles)**: inline-expansion of `nested(plan=\\\"<pack>.<name>\\\")` strings must thread a visiting-set keyed on qualified id; revisit raises `OrchestrateDefinitionError`.\n- **FLAG-006 (argv handles)**: `argv=[..., transcribe.transcript]` is the natural pattern for embedding produces refs. The factory must accept `list[str | _ProducesHandle]` and stringify handles before `shlex.join` \u2014 and tests must cover handles in argv, not just in `repeat_for_each.from_`.\n\nOther practical guidance:\n\n- The DSL's `to_dict()` output must be byte-shape-equivalent to `TaskPlan.to_dict()` so the runtime is unchanged. The cleanest implementation is to construct dicts directly mirroring `artagents/core/task/plan.py` and let `load_plan` validate.\n- Reuse, don't reimplement: import `_strip_artagents_prefix` (or the same logic) for the `code` argv guard; re-export verify helpers verbatim; walk plans with `iter_steps_with_path` for `describe`.\n- The doctor-test flip in T6 is explicitly in scope per the gate's FLAG-001 resolution. Rename the method and change `assertNotIn` \u2192 `assertIn`.\n- `describe` output will be golden-compared in tests \u2014 sort children/keys deterministically.\n- The canonical `builtin.hype` example from doc section 13 may not yet have a DSL module (its DSL migration is a separate task); for the sub-second timing check in T10, use a representative sample plan if hype.py does not exist.\n- The `@orchestrator(plan_id)` decorator is optional sugar; module-level `plan(plan_id, [steps])` must also work to match doc section 13.\n\nThere are no human-only setup actions: this is pure repo-editing work runnable inside the executor's sandbox. No env vars, no infra access, no manual smoke tests.",

AttestedStep)) and step.repeat is not None:\n            if isinstance(step.repeat, RepeatForEach) and step.repeat.from_ref:\n                # load_plan already validated this; emit nothing extra.\n _format_repeat(repeat) -> list[str]:\n    lines: list[str] = []\n    if isinstance(repeat, RepeatUntil):\n        lines.append(f\"repeat.until={repeat.condition}\")\n        lines.append(f\"max_iterations={repeat.max_iterations}\")\n        lines.append(f\"on_exhaust={repeat.on_exhaust}\")\n        if repeat.quorum_n is not None:\n            lines.append(f\"quorum_n={repeat.quorum_n}\")\n    elif isinstance(repeat, RepeatForEach):\n        if repeat.items_source == \"static\":\n            lines.append(f\"for_each items={list(repeat.items)}\")\n        else:\n            lines.append(f\"requires: {repeat.from_ref}\")\n    return lines\n\n\ndef _describe_plan(plan: TaskPlan, builder_costs: dict[str, float]) -> tuple[list[str], float]:\n    out: list[str] = []\n    total_cost = 0.0\n    for path, step in iter_steps_with_path(plan):\n        depth = len(path) - 1\n        indent = \"  \" * depth\n        out.append(f\"{indent}{step.id} [{step.kind}]\")\n        # produces (sorted by name for determinism)\n        for entry in sorted(step.produces, key=lambda e: e.name):\n            out.append(\n                f\"{indent}  produces: {entry.name} -> {entry.path} ({entry.check.check_id})\"\n            )\n        # repeat\n        for line in _format_repeat(step.repeat):\n            out.append(f\"{indent}  {line}\")\n        # cost hint (looked up by step id; collisions across nested trees are\n        # rare and the lookup is best-effort for the footer summary)\n        cost = builder_costs.get(step.id)\n        if cost is not None:\n            total_cost += float(cost)\n    return out, total_cost\n\n\ndef _collect_costs(builder: _PlanBuilder, packs_root: Optional[Path]) -> dict[str, float]:\n    costs: dict[str, float] = {}\n    visiting: set = set()\n\n    def _walk(b: _PlanBuilder) -> None:\n        if b.plan_id in visiting:\n            return\n        visiting.add(b.plan_id)\n        for step in b.steps:\n            if step.cost_hint_usd is not None:\n                costs[step.id] = float(step.cost_hint_usd)\n            child = step.plan\n            if isinstance(child, _PlanBuilder):\n                _walk(child)\n            elif isinstance(child, str):\n                try:\n                    sub = resolve_orchestrator(child, (OrchestrateDefinitionError, TaskPlanError) as exc:\n        _print_err(f\"author describe {qid}: {exc}\")\n        return 1\n    costs = _collect_costs(builder, packs_root)\n    lines, total = _describe_plan(plan, costs)\n    print(f\"plan {plan.plan_id} (version {plan.version})\")\n    for line in lines:\n        print(line)\n    if costs:\n        print(f\"estimated cost ceiling: ${total:.2f}\")\n    return 0\n\n\ndef _cmd_new(qid: str, packs_root: Optional[Path]) -> int:\n    if not _QID_RE.fullmatch(qid):\n        _print_err(\n            f\"author new: qualified id {qid!r} must be '<pack>.<name>' \"\n            \"with letters/digits/underscore\"\n        )\n        return 1\n    pack, name = _qualified_split(qid)\n    root = _packs_root_arg(packs_root)\n    pack_root = root / pack\n    if not pack_root.is_dir():\n        _print_err(\n            f\"author new: pack directory not found at {pack_root}; \"\n            \"create the pack before scaffolding an orchestrator\"\n        )\n        return 1\n    module_path = pack_root / f\"{name}.py\"\n    folder_collision = pack_root / name\n    if module_path.exists():\n        _print_err(f\"author new: refuse to overwrite existing {module_path}\")\n        return 1\n    if folder_collision.exists() and folder_collision.is_dir():\n        # FLAG-003: a same-stem folder shadows the .py module on import.\n        _print_err(\n            f\"author new: cannot scaffold {module_path} because folder \"\n            f\"{folder_collision} exists; rename the folder-orchestrator first\"\n        )\n        return 1\n\n    fixtures_dir = pack_root / \"fixtures\" / name\n    golden_dir = pack_root / \"golden\"\n    fixtures_keep = fixtures_dir / \".keep\"\n    golden_events = golden_dir / f\"{name}.events.jsonl\"\n\n    fixtures_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    golden_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n\n    module_text = _NEW_TEMPLATE.format(qualified_id=qid, fn_name=name)\n    module_path.write_text(module_text, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    fixtures_keep.write_text(\"\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    golden_events.write_text(\"\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n\n    for created in (module_path, fixtures_keep, golden_events):\n        try:\n            rel = created.relative_to(root.parent)\n        except ValueError:\n            rel = created\n        print(f\"created {rel}\")\n    return 0\n\n\ndef _build_parser() -> argparse.ArgumentParser:\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog=\"artagents author\", description=\"Phase 4 author CLI\")\n    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest=\"cmd\", required=True)\n    for verb in (\"compile\", \"check\", \"describe\", \"new\"):\n        sp = sub.add_parser(verb, help=f\"author {verb} <pack>.<name>\")\n        sp.add_argument(\"qualified_id\", help=\"qualified id of the form <pack>.<name>\")\n    return parser\n\n\ndef main(argv: Optional[list] = None, *, packs_root: Optional[Path] = None) -> int:\n    if argv is None:\n        argv = sys.argv[1:]\n    parser = _build_parser()\n    try:\n        args = parser.parse_args(list(argv))\n    except SystemExit as exc:\n        return int(exc.code or 2)\n         Idea:
Implement Phase 4 (Python DSL + verify helpers + author check/describe/new) of the ArtAgents orchestrator V1 design per docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md sections 10 and 14. Phase 4 scope: ship `artagents.orchestrate` Python DSL as the only committed authoring representation. Authors write `<pack>/<orch>.py` using decorators/helpers like `@orchestrator`, `code()`, `attested()`, `nested()`. Step references are typed (e.g., `step.notes` resolves a produces handle, typo-checked at definition). The DSL emits a validated definition equivalent to the JSON manifest schema. Add `artagents author compile` to write the JSON manifest into `<pack>/build/<orch>.json` (gitignored); the runtime hashes/pins this artifact. Add `artagents author check` (sub-second static validation: schema, every produces/requires reference resolves, every nested plan resolves, attested non-trivial produces have semantic checks, code argv to `artagents orchestrators run` is rejected). Add `artagents author describe` (pretty-prints DAG: step kinds, produces, requires, max_iterations, repeat config, estimated cost ceiling if cost hints present). Add `artagents author new <pack>.<name>` (scaffolds Python file + fixtures/<name>/ + golden/<name>.events.jsonl from a template). The verify helper library (artagents/verify/) is already shipped from Phase 3 — re-use file_nonempty, json_schema, json_file, audio_duration_min, image_dimensions, all_of as DSL helpers. Phases 1 (kernel), 2 (three step kinds), 3 (produces + repeat) are committed and shipped — build on top. Do NOT implement new lifecycle verbs (Phase 5). Keep additive: existing JSON manifests and python/command runtimes still work. Tests: typed step reference catches typos at definition; compile produces a valid manifest in build/; check validates references and rejects sentinel-only attested produces; describe pretty-prints expected DAG; new scaffolds correct structure; existing JSON manifests still load and run.

User notes and answers:
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-001 three step kinds: Task mode has exactly `code`, `attested`, and `nested` so execution, evidence, and delegation stay distinct.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-002 repeat body attributes: Iteration and fan-out are `repeat.until` and `repeat.for_each` body attributes so they apply uniformly to steps and nested bodies.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-003 plan naming: Runtime uses immutable `plan.json` instead of checklist naming because the structure is a tree with nested bodies and loops.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-004 event hash chain: `events.jsonl` uses plain `sha256(prev_hash + canonical_event_json)` with no HMAC because V1 needs an edit tripwire, not keyed tamper resistance.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-005 no index database: V1 ships without `.index.db` because file walking and grep over `events.jsonl` are adequate until measured otherwise.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-006 committed DSL: `artagents.orchestrate` Python DSL is the only committed authoring representation so source review happens in Python.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-007 generated build JSON: `<pack>/build/<orch>.json` is gitignored and root `.gitignore` excludes pack build outputs because runtime pins generated artifacts.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-008 per-project CAS: V1 uses only per-project `<slug>/.cas/<sha256>` storage because shared CAS adds coordination without current need.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-009 code boundary: `code` is deterministic argv, a strict superset of `RUNTIME_KINDS={python,command}`, may target `artagents executors run <name>`, and must not target `artagents orchestrators run`.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-010 nested boundary: Sub-orchestrator delegation is exclusively `nested` so the gate can hash and pin child structure.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-011 gate checks: The gate validates active run presence, plan hash, event chain, and incoming command match before dispatch and prints the exact recovery command on rejection.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-012 gate ordering: The gate runs before `_prepare_project_request()` and `thread_wrapper.begin_orchestrator_run()` so rejected commands create zero project-run side effects.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-013 provenance surface: `events.jsonl` is the single write surface for task-mode provenance while thread provenance remains in `runs/<id>/run.json`.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-014 lifecycle dispatch: Task-mode lifecycle verbs extend `artagents/pipeline.py:15` and the existing `artagents orchestrators` CLI remains unchanged.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-015 task kernel namespace: New kernel modules live in `artagents/core/task/` to isolate gate, events, active-run, and env logic from legacy runners.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-016 structure guardrails: `TOP_LEVEL_ARTAGENTS_DIRS` adds `orchestrate` and `verify`, with `tests/test_doctor_setup.py` updated so doctor accepts the new packages.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-017 task run env: `ARTAGENTS_TASK_RUN_ID` is the integration surface for orchestrator, executor, and hype child calls to attach to the parent task run.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-018 child output preservation: Task mode preserves child output dirs and mirrors hype artifacts under `runs/<task-run-id>/steps/<step-id>/produces/` while standalone behavior stays unchanged so `tests/test_project_runs.py` keeps passing.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-019 attestor identity: Agent attestations require `--agent`, human attestations require `--actor` matching `ARTAGENTS_ACTOR`, and self-acks are rejected.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-020 ack rules: `approve` advances, `retry` is only valid after verifier failure, `iterate --feedback` is only valid for `repeat.until=user_approves`, and `abort` ends the run.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-021 inline produces checks: Produces inline checks replace verifier substeps while `passes_test` remains a normal `code` step.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-022 semantic checks: `author check` rejects sentinel-only attested outputs because non-trivial artifacts need semantic validation.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-023 stop-hook preamble: Every `artagents next` output includes the prohibition preamble verbatim because repeated injection fights context decay.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-024 additive migration: Existing `OrchestratorDefinition` JSON manifests and `RUNTIME_KINDS={python,command}` keep working because V1 migration is additive.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-025 honor model: V1 is single-host and honor-based with strong logging because it does not defend against malicious local agents.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-026 cut points: Phase 3 is the frozen-plan runner ship point and Phase 5 is the author-friendly lifecycle ship point.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-027 canonical migration: `artagents/packs/builtin/hype/` is the canonical migration example because it exercises the existing executor pipeline and additive pack layout.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-028 phasing order: V1 implementation follows build phases 1-9 so kernel, step semantics, authoring, lifecycle, nudge, CAS, inbox, and golden tests land in dependency order.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-029 V1 infrastructure limits: V1 excludes daemon, web server, HMAC/keyed crypto, `.index.db`, and shared CAS so the implementation stays file-based, inspectable, and additive.

  "Cost-hint extraction: cost_hint_usd is stored on _StepHandle (DSL-only attribute, never serialized to the load_plan-validated payload), so describe walks the original _PlanBuilder tree (not the TaskPlan) to collect cost hints. Match between builder step ids and TaskPlan step ids is by id; collisions across nested subtrees pick the last-walked value, which is acceptable for an estimate footer. The footer is omitted entirely when no step declares a hint, per spec.",
  "check command's explicit author-friendly checks largely overlap with what to_dict + load_plan already enforce (DSL constructors reject orchestrators-run argv and reserved-attr produces; load_plan rejects sentinel-only attested produces and validates repeat.for_each.from; the resolver expands nested string refs and raises on missing modules). The CLI relies on these established validators rather than re-implementing parallel checks; it adds a redundant pre-order walk to surface a clear pass message and to honor the spec's enumeration.   "meta_commentary": "Phase 4 ships the only committed authoring surface for V1 task plans: a tiny Python DSL at `artagents/orchestrate/` plus a top-level `author` CLI verb. Phase 4 is strictly additive \u2014 no schema changes, no lifecycle verbs (those are Phase 5), and existing `orchestrator.yaml` JSON manifests must keep working. Critical execution notes for the gate-flagged concerns:\n\n- **FLAG-002 (private validator)**: do NOT import `_validate_plan` from `artagents.core.task.plan`. Round-trip the DSL payload by writing to a temp file and calling the public `load_plan(path)`. The plan text says \"call `_validate_plan`\"; this finalize overrides that to use the public symbol.\n- **FLAG-003 (scaffold collision)**: `artagents/packs/builtin/hype/` exists as a folder-orchestrator. `author new` must refuse to write `<pack>/<name>.py` when `<pack>/<name>/` already exists, not just when the same `.py` file exists. Python's package-shadows-module rule would otherwise make the scaffolded DSL unimportable.\n- **FLAG-004 (`__getattr__` collisions)**: produces names that collide with real `_StepHandle` attributes (`id`, `kind`, `produces`, `repeat`, `command`, `argv`, `plan`, `instructions`, `ack`) silently bypass the typo trap. Reject these at construction.\n- **FLAG-005 (nested cycles)**: inline-expansion of `nested(plan=\\\"<pack>.<name>\\\")` strings must thread a visiting-set keyed on qualified id; revisit raises `OrchestrateDefinitionError`.\n- **FLAG-006 (argv handles)**: `argv=[..., transcribe.transcript]` is the natural pattern for embedding produces refs. The factory must accept `list[str | _ProducesHandle]` and stringify handles before `shlex.join` \u2014 and tests must cover handles in argv, not just in `repeat_for_each.from_`.\n\nOther practical guidance:\n\n- The DSL's `to_dict()` output must be byte-shape-equivalent to `TaskPlan.to_dict()` so the runtime is unchanged. The cleanest implementation is to construct dicts directly mirroring `artagents/core/task/plan.py` and let `load_plan` validate.\n- Reuse, don't reimplement: import `_strip_artagents_prefix` (or the same logic) for the `code` argv guard; re-export verify helpers verbatim; walk plans with `iter_steps_with_path` for `describe`.\n- The doctor-test flip in T6 is explicitly in scope per the gate's FLAG-001 resolution. Rename the method and change `assertNotIn` \u2192 `assertIn`.\n- `describe` output will be golden-compared in tests \u2014 sort children/keys deterministically.\n- The canonical `builtin.hype` example from doc section 13 may not yet have a DSL module (its DSL migration is a separate task); for the sub-second timing check in T10, use a representative sample plan if hype.py does not exist.\n- The `@orchestrator(plan_id)` decorator is optional sugar; module-level `plan(plan_id, [steps])` must also work to match doc section 13.\n\nThere are no human-only setup actions: this is pure repo-editing work runnable inside the executor's sandbox. No env vars, no infra access, no manual smoke tests.",

1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""ArtAgents top-level command gateway.
3	
4	Subcommands dispatch to focused module CLIs (executors, orchestrators,
5	elements, projects, threads, modalities, doctor, setup, audit). Brief / video
6	flags fall through to the ``builtin.hype`` orchestrator resolved through the
7	orchestrator registry.
8	"""
9	
10	from __future__ import annotations
11	
12	import sys
13	
14	
15	def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
16	    raw = sys.argv[1:] if argv is None else list(argv)
17	    if raw and raw[0] in {"-h", "--help"}:
18	        _print_entrypoint_help()
19	        return 0
20	    project_slug = _extract_project_slug(raw)
21	    if project_slug is None:
22	        return _dispatch(raw)
23	
24	    from .core.task import gate as task_gate
25	
26	    try:
27	        decision = task_gate.gate_command(project_slug, task_gate.command_for_argv(raw), raw)
28	    except task_gate.TaskRunGateError as exc:
29	        print(f"task-mode gate rejected: {exc.reason}\nrecovery: {exc.recovery}", file=sys.stderr)
30	        return 1
31	    if not decision.active:
32	        return _dispatch(raw)
33	
34	    returncode = -1
35	    try:
36	        returncode = _dispatch(raw)
37	        return returncode
38	    finally:
39	        task_gate.record_dispatch_complete(decision, returncode)
40	
41	
42	def _dispatch(raw: list[str]) -> int:
43	    if raw and raw[0] == "publish":
44	        from .packs.builtin.publish import run as publish
45	
46	        return publish.main(raw[1:])
47	    if raw and raw[0] == "publish-youtube":
48	        from .packs.upload.youtube import run as publish_youtube
49	
50	        return publish_youtube.main(raw[1:])
51	    if raw and raw[0] == "upload-youtube":
52	        from .packs.upload.youtube import run as publish_youtube
53	
54	        return publish_youtube.main(raw[1:])
55	    if raw and raw[0] == "executors":
56	        from .core.executor import cli as executors_cli
57	
58	        return executors_cli.main(raw[1:])
59	    if raw and raw[0] == "orchestrators":
60	        from .core.orchestrator import cli as orchestrators_cli
61	
62	        return orchestrators_cli.main(raw[1:])

65	
66	        return elements_cli.main(raw[1:])

69

73

102	def _extract_project_slug(raw: list[str]) -> str | None:
103	    for index, token in enumerate(raw):
104	        if token == "--project":
105	            return raw[index + 1] if index + 1 < len(raw) else None
106	        if token.startswith("--project="):
107	            value = token.split("=", 1)[1]
108	            return value or None
109	    return None
110	
111	
112	def _run_default_brief_orchestrator(argv: list[str]) -> int:
113	    from importlib import import_module
114	
115	    from .core.orchestrator.registry import load_default_registry
116	
117	    registry = load_default_registry()
118	    orchestrator = registry.get("builtin.hype")
119	    runtime_module = orchestrator.metadata.get("runtime_module")
120	    runtime_entrypoint = orchestrator.metadata.get("runtime_entrypoint", "main")
121	    if not isinstance(runtime_module, str) or not runtime_module:
122	        raise RuntimeError("builtin.hype manifest is missing metadata.runtime_module")
123	    module = import_module(runtime_module)
124	    entrypoint = getattr(module, runtime_entrypoint)
125	    return int(entrypoint(argv))
126

137	  python3 -m artagents elements {list,inspect,fork,install} ...

139	  python3 -m artagents thread {new,list,show,archive,reopen,backfill,keep,dismiss,group} ...

141	  python3 -m artagents reigh-data --project-id PROJECT_ID [--out PATH]
142	  python3 -m artagents worker --pool banodoco [--worker-id ID] [--max-iterations N]
143	  python3 -m artagents audit --run RUN_DIR
144	  python3 -m artagents --video SRC --brief BRIEF --out runs/name [--render]
145	  python3 -m artagents --brief BRIEF --out runs/name --target-duration SECONDS [--render]
146	Start here:
147	  python3 -m artagents doctor
148	  python3 -m artagents orchestrators list
149	  python3 -m artagents executors list
150	  python3 -m artagents elements list
151	  python3 -m artagents projects show --project PROJECT
152	  python3 -m artagents thread list
153	  python3 -m artagents modalities list
154	
155	Inspect before running:
156	  python3 -m artagents orchestrators inspect builtin.hype --json
157	  python3 -m artagents executors inspect builtin.render --json
158	  python3 -m artagents elements inspect effects text-card --json

160	
161	Run any tool through this gateway:

164	
165	Notes:
166	  python3 -m artagents is the package entry point.
167	  Use orchestrators for workflows, executors for concrete work, and elements for render building blocks.
168	"""
169	    )
170	
171	
172	if __name__ == "__main__":

Phase 4 scope: ship `artagents.orchestrate` Python DSL as the only committed authoring representation. Authors write `<pack>/<orch>.py` using decorators/helpers like `@orchestrator`, `code()`, `attested()`, `nested()`. Step references are typed (e.g., `step.notes` resolves a produces handle, typo-checked at definition). The DSL emits a validated definition equivalent to the JSON manifest schema. Add `artagents author compile` to write the JSON manifest into `<pack>/build/<orch>.json` (gitignored); the runtime hashes/pins this artifact. Add `artagents author check` (sub-second static validation: schema, every produces/requires reference resolves, every nested plan resolves, attested non-trivial produces have semantic checks, code argv to `artagents orchestrators run` is rejected). Add `artagents author describe` (pretty-prints DAG: step kinds, produces, requires, max_iterations, repeat config, estimated cost ceiling if cost hints present). Add `artagents author new <pack>.<name>` (scaffolds Python file + fixtures/<name>/ + golden/<name>.events.jsonl from a template). The verify helper library (artagents/verify/) is already shipped from Phase 3 — re-use file_nonempty, json_schema, json_file, audio_duration_min, image_dimensions, all_of as DSL helpers. Phases 1 (kernel), 2 (three step kinds), 3 (produces + repeat) are committed and shipped — build on top. Do NOT implement new lifecycle verbs (Phase 5). Keep additive: existing JSON manifests and python/command runtimes still work. Tests: typed step reference catches typos at definition; compile produces a valid manifest in build/; check validates references and rejects sentinel-only attested produces; describe pretty-prints expected DAG; new scaffolds correct structure; existing JSON manifests still load and run.

User notes and answers:
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-001 three step kinds: Task mode has exactly `code`, `attested`, and `nested` so execution, evidence, and delegation stay distinct.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-002 repeat body attributes: Iteration and fan-out are `repeat.until` and `repeat.for_each` body attributes so they apply uniformly to steps and nested bodies.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-003 plan naming: Runtime uses immutable `plan.json` instead of checklist naming because the structure is a tree with nested bodies and loops.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-004 event hash chain: `events.jsonl` uses plain `sha256(prev_hash + canonical_event_json)` with no HMAC because V1 needs an edit tripwire, not keyed tamper resistance.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-005 no index database: V1 ships without `.index.db` because file walking and grep over `events.jsonl` are adequate until measured otherwise.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-006 committed DSL: `artagents.orchestrate` Python DSL is the only committed authoring representation so source review happens in Python.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-007 generated build JSON: `<pack>/build/<orch>.json` is gitignored and root `.gitignore` excludes pack build outputs because runtime pins generated artifacts.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-008 per-project CAS: V1 uses only per-project `<slug>/.cas/<sha256>` storage because shared CAS adds coordination without current need.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-009 code boundary: `code` is deterministic argv, a strict superset of `RUNTIME_KINDS={python,command}`, may target `artagents executors run <name>`, and must not target `artagents orchestrators run`.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-010 nested boundary: Sub-orchestrator delegation is exclusively `nested` so the gate can hash and pin child structure.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-011 gate checks: The gate validates active run presence, plan hash, event chain, and incoming command match before dispatch and prints the exact recovery command on rejection.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-012 gate ordering: The gate runs before `_prepare_project_request()` and `thread_wrapper.begin_orchestrator_run()` so rejected commands create zero project-run side effects.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-013 provenance surface: `events.jsonl` is the single write surface for task-mode provenance while thread provenance remains in `runs/<id>/run.json`.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-014 lifecycle dispatch: Task-mode lifecycle verbs extend `artagents/pipeline.py:15` and the existing `artagents orchestrators` CLI remains unchanged.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-015 task kernel namespace: New kernel modules live in `artagents/core/task/` to isolate gate, events, active-run, and env logic from legacy runners.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-016 structure guardrails: `TOP_LEVEL_ARTAGENTS_DIRS` adds `orchestrate` and `verify`, with `tests/test_doctor_setup.py` updated so doctor accepts the new packages.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-017 task run env: `ARTAGENTS_TASK_RUN_ID` is the integration surface for orchestrator, executor, and hype child calls to attach to the parent task run.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-018 child output preservation: Task mode preserves child output dirs and mirrors hype artifacts under `runs/<task-run-id>/steps/<step-id>/produces/` while standalone behavior stays unchanged so `tests/test_project_runs.py` keeps passing.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-019 attestor identity: Agent attestations require `--agent`, human attestations require `--actor` matching `ARTAGENTS_ACTOR`, and self-acks are rejected.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-020 ack rules: `approve` advances, `retry` is only valid after verifier failure, `iterate --feedback` is only valid for `repeat.until=user_approves`, and `abort` ends the run.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-021 inline produces checks: Produces inline checks replace verifier substeps while `passes_test` remains a normal `code` step.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-022 semantic checks: `author check` rejects sentinel-only attested outputs because non-trivial artifacts need semantic validation.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-023 stop-hook preamble: Every `artagents next` output includes the prohibition preamble verbatim because repeated injection fights context decay.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-024 additive migration: Existing `OrchestratorDefinition` JSON manifests and `RUNTIME_KINDS={python,command}` keep working because V1 migration is additive.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-025 honor model: V1 is single-host and honor-based with strong logging because it does not defend against malicious local agents.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-026 cut points: Phase 3 is the frozen-plan runner ship point and Phase 5 is the author-friendly lifecycle ship point.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-027 canonical migration: `artagents/packs/builtin/hype/` is the canonical migration example because it exercises the existing executor pipeline and additive pack layout.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-028 phasing order: V1 implementation follows build phases 1-9 so kernel, step semantics, authoring, lifecycle, nudge, CAS, inbox, and golden tests land in dependency order.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-029 V1 infrastructure limits: V1 excludes daemon, web server, HMAC/keyed crypto, `.index.db`, and shared CAS so the implementation stays file-based, inspectable, and additive.

  "meta_commentary": "Phase 4 ships the only committed authoring surface for V1 task plans: a tiny Python DSL at `artagents/orchestrate/` plus a top-level `author` CLI verb. Phase 4 is strictly additive \u2014 no schema changes, no lifecycle verbs (those are Phase 5), and existing `orchestrator.yaml` JSON manifests must keep working. Critical execution notes for the gate-flagged concerns:\n\n- **FLAG-002 (private validator)**: do NOT import `_validate_plan` from `artagents.core.task.plan`. Round-trip the DSL payload by writing to a temp file and calling the public `load_plan(path)`. The plan text says \"call `_validate_plan`\"; this finalize overrides that to use the public symbol.\n- **FLAG-003 (scaffold collision)**: `artagents/packs/builtin/hype/` exists as a folder-orchestrator. `author new` must refuse to write `<pack>/<name>.py` when `<pack>/<name>/` already exists, not just when the same `.py` file exists. Python's package-shadows-module rule would otherwise make the scaffolded DSL unimportable.\n- **FLAG-004 (`__getattr__` collisions)**: produces names that collide with real `_StepHandle` attributes (`id`, `kind`, `produces`, `repeat`, `command`, `argv`, `plan`, `instructions`, `ack`) silently bypass the typo trap. Reject these at construction.\n- **FLAG-005 (nested cycles)**: inline-expansion of `nested(plan=\\\"<pack>.<name>\\\")` strings must thread a visiting-set keyed on qualified id; revisit raises `OrchestrateDefinitionError`.\n- **FLAG-006 (argv handles)**: `argv=[..., transcribe.transcript]` is the natural pattern for embedding produces refs. The factory must accept `list[str | _ProducesHandle]` and stringify handles before `shlex.join` \u2014 and tests must cover handles in argv, not just in `repeat_for_each.from_`.\n\nOther practical guidance:\n\n- The DSL's `to_dict()` output must be byte-shape-equivalent to `TaskPlan.to_dict()` so the runtime is unchanged. The cleanest implementation is to construct dicts directly mirroring `artagents/core/task/plan.py` and let `load_plan` validate.\n- Reuse, don't reimplement: import `_strip_artagents_prefix` (or the same logic) for the `code` argv guard; re-export verify helpers verbatim; walk plans with `iter_steps_with_path` for `describe`.\n- The doctor-test flip in T6 is explicitly in scope per the gate's FLAG-001 resolution. Rename the method and change `assertNotIn` \u2192 `assertIn`.\n- `describe` output will be golden-compared in tests \u2014 sort children/keys deterministically.\n- The canonical `builtin.hype` example from doc section 13 may not yet have a DSL module (its DSL migration is a separate task); for the sub-second timing check in T10, use a representative sample plan if hype.py does not exist.\n- The `@orchestrator(plan_id)` decorator is optional sugar; module-level `plan(plan_id, [steps])` must also work to match doc section 13.\n\nThere are no human-only setup actions: this is pure repo-editing work runnable inside the executor's sandbox. No env vars, no infra access, no manual smoke tests.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked v5's worker auth story against `/users/user_c042661f/documents/banodoco-workspace/banodoco-worker/worker_jwt.py` and `worker.py`. `worker_jwt.verify_user_jwt` validates signature/audience/expiry and returns the jwt subject; it does not prove project or timeline ownership. the reference worker separately calls `_verify_project_ownership(rpc, project_id, verified.user_id)` via a service-role read of `projects.user_id` before writing. v5 says `worker_jwt.py` confirms the user owns the timeline and only tests missing ownership claims, but it does not add the separate project-ownership check, which is required when the terminal write uses service-role. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] auth-scope: auth scope: v5 broadens service-role timeline writes from the worker-only path into generic supabasedataprovider, open_in_reigh, and project cli edit flows, conflicting with sd-009's user-jwt/pat ownership-bound cli model. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked v5 against sd-009 and the corrected user notes. the corrected facts only establish the service-role rpc path for the trusted aa worker, but v5 makes the generic `supabasedataprovider.save_timeline`, `open_in_reigh` default flow, and project cli edit verbs all take `service_role_key` and call the versioned rpc. that broadens service-role use beyond the worker and conflicts with the brief's user-jwt/pat ownership-bound cli path. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the reference worker call chain for write authorization. in `banodoco-worker/worker.py`, after jwt verification the caller creates `supabasetimelinerpc(audited_user_id=verified.user_id)` and then calls `_verify_project_ownership(rpc, project_id, verified.user_id)` before the service-role write. v5's worker caller path lacks that explicit ownership-verification call and relies on `worker_jwt.py` to prove ownership, which the reference `worker_jwt.py` does not do. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] package-source-path-references: package source path references: v5 fixes package-root file dependencies, but phase 0 step 1 still names package-root `src/index.ts` files that do not exist; the actual source files are under `typescript/src/index.ts`. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

Phase 4 scope: ship `artagents.orchestrate` Python DSL as the only committed authoring representation. Authors write `<pack>/<orch>.py` using decorators/helpers like `@orchestrator`, `code()`, `attested()`, `nested()`. Step references are typed (e.g., `step.notes` resolves a produces handle, typo-checked at definition). The DSL emits a validated definition equivalent to the JSON manifest schema. Add `artagents author compile` to write the JSON manifest into `<pack>/build/<orch>.json` (gitignored); the runtime hashes/pins this artifact. Add `artagents author check` (sub-second static validation: schema, every produces/requires reference resolves, every nested plan resolves, attested non-trivial produces have semantic checks, code argv to `artagents orchestrators run` is rejected). Add `artagents author describe` (pretty-prints DAG: step kinds, produces, requires, max_iterations, repeat config, estimated cost ceiling if cost hints present). Add `artagents author new <pack>.<name>` (scaffolds Python file + fixtures/<name>/ + golden/<name>.events.jsonl from a template). The verify helper library (artagents/verify/) is already shipped from Phase 3 — re-use file_nonempty, json_schema, json_file, audio_duration_min, image_dimensions, all_of as DSL helpers. Phases 1 (kernel), 2 (three step kinds), 3 (produces + repeat) are committed and shipped — build on top. Do NOT implement new lifecycle verbs (Phase 5). - Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-001 three step kinds: Task mode has exactly `code`, `attested`, and `nested` so execution, evidence, and delegation stay distinct.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-002 repeat body attributes: Iteration and fan-out are `repeat.until` and `repeat.for_each` body attributes so they apply uniformly to steps and nested bodies.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-003 plan naming: Runtime uses immutable `plan.json` instead of checklist naming because the structure is a tree with nested bodies and loops.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-004 event hash chain: `events.jsonl` uses plain `sha256(prev_hash + canonical_event_json)` with no HMAC because V1 needs an edit tripwire, not keyed tamper resistance.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-005 no index database: V1 ships without `.index.db` because file walking and grep over `events.jsonl` are adequate until measured otherwise.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-006 committed DSL: `artagents.orchestrate` Python DSL is the only committed authoring representation so source review happens in Python.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-007 generated build JSON: `<pack>/build/<orch>.json` is gitignored and root `.gitignore` excludes pack build outputs because runtime pins generated artifacts.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-008 per-project CAS: V1 uses only per-project `<slug>/.cas/<sha256>` storage because shared CAS adds coordination without current need.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-009 code boundary: `code` is deterministic argv, a strict superset of `RUNTIME_KINDS={python,command}`, may target `artagents executors run <name>`, and must not target `artagents orchestrators run`.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-010 nested boundary: Sub-orchestrator delegation is exclusively `nested` so the gate can hash and pin child structure.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-011 gate checks: The gate validates active run presence, plan hash, event chain, and incoming command match before dispatch and prints the exact recovery command on rejection.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-012 gate ordering: The gate runs before `_prepare_project_request()` and `thread_wrapper.begin_orchestrator_run()` so rejected commands create zero project-run side effects.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-013 provenance surface: `events.jsonl` is the single write surface for task-mode provenance while thread provenance remains in `runs/<id>/run.json`.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-014 lifecycle dispatch: Task-mode lifecycle verbs extend `artagents/pipeline.py:15` and the existing `artagents orchestrators` CLI remains unchanged.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-015 task kernel namespace: New kernel modules live in `artagents/core/task/` to isolate gate, events, active-run, and env logic from legacy runners.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-016 structure guardrails: `TOP_LEVEL_ARTAGENTS_DIRS` adds `orchestrate` and `verify`, with `tests/test_doctor_setup.py` updated so doctor accepts the new packages.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-017 task run env: `ARTAGENTS_TASK_RUN_ID` is the integration surface for orchestrator, executor, and hype child calls to attach to the parent task run.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-018 child output preservation: Task mode preserves child output dirs and mirrors hype artifacts under `runs/<task-run-id>/steps/<step-id>/produces/` while standalone behavior stays unchanged so `tests/test_project_runs.py` keeps passing.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-019 attestor identity: Agent attestations require `--agent`, human attestations require `--actor` matching `ARTAGENTS_ACTOR`, and self-acks are rejected.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-020 ack rules: `approve` advances, `retry` is only valid after verifier failure, `iterate --feedback` is only valid for `repeat.until=user_approves`, and `abort` ends the run.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-021 inline produces checks: Produces inline checks replace verifier substeps while `passes_test` remains a normal `code` step.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-022 semantic checks: `author check` rejects sentinel-only attested outputs because non-trivial artifacts need semantic validation.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-023 stop-hook preamble: Every `artagents next` output includes the prohibition preamble verbatim because repeated injection fights context decay.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-024 additive migration: Existing `OrchestratorDefinition` JSON manifests and `RUNTIME_KINDS={python,command}` keep working because V1 migration is additive.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-025 honor model: V1 is single-host and honor-based with strong logging because it does not defend against malicious local agents.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-026 cut points: Phase 3 is the frozen-plan runner ship point and Phase 5 is the author-friendly lifecycle ship point.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-027 canonical migration: `artagents/packs/builtin/hype/` is the canonical migration example because it exercises the existing executor pipeline and additive pack layout.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-028 phasing order: V1 implementation follows build phases 1-9 so kernel, step semantics, authoring, lifecycle, nudge, CAS, inbox, and golden tests land in dependency order.
- Ignored malformed settled decision line: SD-029 V1 infrastructure limits: V1 excludes daemon, web server, HMAC/keyed crypto, `.index.db`, and shared CAS so the implementation stays file-based, inspectable, and additive.

  "user_actions": [],
  "meta_commentary": "Phase 4 ships the only committed authoring surface for V1 task plans: a tiny Python DSL at `artagents/orchestrate/` plus a top-level `author` CLI verb. Phase 4 is strictly additive \u2014 no schema changes, no lifecycle verbs (those are Phase 5), and existing `orchestrator.yaml` JSON manifests must keep working. Critical execution notes for the gate-flagged concerns:\n\n- **FLAG-002 (private validator)**: do NOT import `_validate_plan` from `artagents.core.task.plan`. Round-trip the DSL payload by writing to a temp file and calling the public `load_plan(path)`. The plan text says \"call `_validate_plan`\"; this finalize overrides that to use the public symbol.\n- **FLAG-003 (scaffold collision)**: `artagents/packs/builtin/hype/` exists as a folder-orchestrator. `author new` must refuse to write `<pack>/<name>.py` when `<pack>/<name>/` already exists, not just when the same `.py` file exists. Python's package-shadows-module rule would otherwise make the scaffolded DSL unimportable.\n- **FLAG-004 (`__getattr__` collisions)**: produces names that collide with real `_StepHandle` attributes (`id`, `kind`, `produces`, `repeat`, `command`, `argv`, `plan`, `instructions`, `ack`) silently bypass the typo trap. Reject these at construction.\n- **FLAG-005 (nested cycles)**: inline-expansion of `nested(plan=\\\"<pack>.<name>\\\")` strings must thread a visiting-set keyed on qualified id; revisit raises `OrchestrateDefinitionError`.\n- **FLAG-006 (argv handles)**: `argv=[..., transcribe.transcript]` is the natural pattern for embedding produces refs. The factory must accept `list[str | _ProducesHandle]` and stringify handles before `shlex.join` \u2014 and tests must cover handles in argv, not just in `repeat_for_each.from_`.\n\nOther practical guidance:\n\n- The DSL's `to_dict()` output must be byte-shape-equivalent to `TaskPlan.to_dict()` so the runtime is unchanged. The cleanest implementation is to construct dicts directly mirroring `artagents/core/task/plan.py` and let `load_plan` validate.\n- Reuse, don't reimplement: import `_strip_artagents_prefix` (or the same logic) for the `code` argv guard; re-export verify helpers verbatim; walk plans with `iter_steps_with_path` for `describe`.\n- The doctor-test flip in T6 is explicitly in scope per the gate's FLAG-001 resolution. Rename the method and change `assertNotIn` \u2192 `assertIn`.\n- `describe` output will be golden-compared in tests \u2014 sort children/keys deterministically.\n- The canonical `builtin.hype` example from doc section 13 may not yet have a DSL module (its DSL migration is a separate task); for the sub-second timing check in T10, use a representative sample plan if hype.py does not exist.\n- The `@orchestrator(plan_id)` decorator is optional sugar; module-level `plan(plan_id, [steps])` must also work to match doc section 13.\n\nThere are no human-only setup actions: this is pure repo-editing work runnable inside the executor's sandbox. No env vars, no infra access, no manual smoke tests.",

{
  "file_path": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/_t10_timing.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Throwaway: time `author check` on a hype-shaped sample plan.\n\nMirrors the canonical `builtin.hype` example from\ndocs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md section 13 (transcribe -> cut -> render ->\nnested thumbnail). The actual `builtin.hype` DSL module does not exist\nyet (its migration is a separate task), so this script writes an\nequivalent plan into a temp pack and exercises the CLI.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport tempfile\nimport time\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom artagents.orchestrate import cli as author_cli\n\n\nSAMPLE_HYPE = '''from artagents.orchestrate import (\n    code,\n    file_nonempty,\n    json_file,\n    nested,\n    orchestrator,\n)\n\n\n@orchestrator(\"sample.hype\")\ndef hype():\n    transcribe = code(\n        \"transcribe\",\n        argv=[\"python3\", \"-m\", \"artagents\", \"executors\", \"run\", \"builtin.transcribe\"],\n        produces={\"transcript\": json_file()},\n    )\n    cut = code(\n        \"cut\",\n        argv=[\"python3\", \"-m\", \"artagents\", \"executors\", \"run\", \"builtin.cut\"],\n        produces={\"timeline\": json_file(), \"assets\": json_file()},\n    )\n    render = code(\n        \"render\",\n        argv=[\"python3\", \"-m\", \"artagents\", \"executors\", \"run\", \"builtin.render\"],\n        produces={\"video\": file_nonempty()},\n    )\n    return [transcribe, cut, render]\n'''\n\nSAMPLE_THUMBNAIL = '''from artagents.orchestrate import code, file_nonempty, orchestrator\n\n\n@orchestrator(\"sample.thumbnail_maker\")\ndef thumbnail_maker():\n    return [\n        code(\n            \"make\",\n            argv=[\"python3\", \"-m\", \"artagents\", \"executors\", \"run\", \"builtin.thumbnail\"],\n            produces={\"thumbnail\": file_nonempty()},\n        ),\n    ]\n'''\n\n\ndef main() -> int:\n    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:\n        packs = Path(tmp) / \"packs\" / \"sample\"\n        packs.mkdir(parents=True)\n        (packs / \"hype.py\").write_text(SAMPLE_HYPE, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        (packs / \"thumbnail_maker.py\").write_text(SAMPLE_THUMBNAIL, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n\n        # Warm-up import (one-time module-load cost; the SLA targets check\n        # work, not Python's startup latency).\n        rc_warm = author_cli.main([\"check\", \"sample.hype\"], packs_root=packs.parent)\n        assert rc_warm == 0\n\n        started = time.perf_counter()\n        rc = author_cli.main([\"check\", \"sample.hype\"], packs_root=packs.parent)\n        elapsed_ms = (time.perf_counter() - started) * 1000.0\n        assert rc == 0\n        print(f\"author check sample.hype took {elapsed_ms:.1f} ms (sub-second SLA)\")\n        assert elapsed_ms < 1000.0, f\"check exceeded 1000 ms SLA: {elapsed_ms}\"\n    return 0\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    raise SystemExit(main())\n"
}
